% what an examiner's rubric looks for).
\subsection{Research gap}
\label{subsec:research-gap}

% REBUILT 2026-09-08 on Marc's rulings (handoff §11; his dictation the
% same hour). Shape: P1 the verdict (DICTATED: the table read across,
% the consequence in the post-ingress framing hedged to properties 1
% and 4, then the RoA sentence compressed by deletion); P2 the means
% (ported, two paragraphs compressed to one by deletion); P3 the close
% (DICTATED: the forward link to ch4 --- Marc RE-RULED the standing

% §5): "we've motivated our attacker model, which is defined in Chapter
% 4"; ch4's first sentence makes the same link back). CUT: the roll-call
% placeholder (absorbed into P1); "Seen from the attacker's side, the
% gap sharpens" (no antecedent since the port); "choosing the
% computationally best move without modelling whether a real attacker
% could, or would, make it. That, in other words, is rationality
% without capability." (descriptor residue --- the review's verdict on
% the parametric rung; the vivid line retired, revivable from git);

% stale "3.2 closer" pointer: the scored-over claim is 3.2.2's last
% sentence (hong2018); Marc did not dictate the scissors clause (§11.1)
% --- open, his call.
% PASS 4 APPLIED 2026-09-08 (split-stream, handoff §11.7, Marc: "accept
% all"). M1: property 2 added to the antecedent; the partial-not-the-
% definition clause stated once (from the caption decode); the two cost
% legs PORTED from apt_model_criterion.md l.194--198 (axis 1, Alshamrani
% §IV-C-2-B) and l.389 (axis 4) --- session-ported, ratify on read;
% "unmeasured" scoped to the table's works. M2: "defender-computed"
% dropped (Brown's RoA is the attacker's own ordering signal); hong2018
% here replaced by a back-pointer to 3.2.2 (the [VERIFY] on hong2018
% stands AT THE 3.2.2 SITE); the game-theoretic sentence CUT ---
% reversible, SUPERSEDED: "In the game-theoretic approaches the attacker
% model competes with the defence directly \citep[Sec.~VI-A]{cho2020},
% so the metrics a defender and an attacker produce take the weaker of
% the two." (3.2.1 carries the competition point, l.~645--653; the
% "weaker of the two" step is unwalked and its mechanism is Marc's to
% dictate if wanted); "and efficiency" dropped (Hong's efficiency family
% is defender-side). "weakest link" KEPT by choice: the discussion's use
% of the same phrase for the movement attacker's own action layer is the
% deliberate symmetry (movement_objectives thread) --- the ch6 sentence
% should point back here. M3: Rodríguez re-glossed (runtime telemetry);
% the CVSS contrast named to the lineage and Masud (Kim has none);
% "validated" cut; "never" scoped to the evaluations surveyed; the
% negative-scope sentence and the hand-off clause ("does not formally
% define", rq_and_structure l.53--54, Jalowski p.~8) session-ported,
% ratify on read.
% PASS 5 APPLIED 2026-09-08 (handoff §11.8; Marc: "do class A, class B,
% not class C"): C1 the partial clause (carrier: Table 3.3 caption), C2
% the MTD-value gloss (carrier: the Alshamrani cite, criterion
% l.194--198), C15 the metrics-as-result sentence (carrier: 3.2.2's
% closing sentence; S6's because-clause keeps it here), C8, C10 the CVSS
% contrast (carrier: 3.3.2 P2 (6), P3), C17, C9 the CAPEC clause
% (carrier: bland2020.md l.47), C18 the tricolon opener, C21/C22. C6 not
% applied (exclusive with C15). Class C (C19 the surveys tail, C20 the
% hand-off clause) REJECTED --- do not re-flag. Residual overdraft
% claimed at the §3.3 reconciliation.
% DRAFT STATE (2026-09-08): P1 and P3 DICTATED by Marc, repaired (pass
% 2+3a); [3b] RESOLVED 2026-09-08 (Marc: ``partially fulfils'',
% ``remains unmeasured''; *partial* ratified over *stand-in* thesis-wide,
% both captions and both count sentences converted); P2 and the RoA
% sentence deletion-only
% compressions of ported text, ratify on read.
The MTDSim lineage partially fulfils six of the eight properties,
Masud et al.\ none, and Kim et al.\ two
(Table~\ref{tab:attacker-cross-section}). An attacker model
without properties 1, 2 and 4 does not model an attack campaign after
initial access: an attacker with no multi-stage knowledge to lose
cannot register the value MTD claims
\citep[Sec.~IV-C-2-B]{alshamrani2019}, and an attacker that cannot adapt overstates MTD's effect. The
performance of MTD against an attacker with a foothold therefore
remains unmeasured in the works of Table~\ref{tab:attacker-cross-section}.
Where
these models grant the attacker any rationality, it is a heuristic
such as RoA \citep{brown2023}, optimised over exploits the attacker
cannot sequence, adapt, or remember.
% SCISSORS CLAUSE DICTATED 2026-09-08 (Marc), repaired per pass 2+3a;
% [3b] RESOLVED 2026-09-08 (Marc accepted both): Cho Sec. VI-A (Game
% Theory-Based MTD; the decision-utility pro, source l.502) on the
% game-theoretic sentence; the passive kept. Raw in the handoff §11.
The assumptions about the attacker are baked into the logic of the
defender (Section~\ref{subsec:mtd-metrics}). Over the
effectiveness metrics the attacker is the weakest link: with a weak
attacker model the defence mechanisms cannot be evaluated,
because the metrics are intertwined and computed
over the attacker model.

The means to close the gap already exist in MTD-adjacent literatures.
Attack profiling recovers TTP-level behaviour from raw CTI
\citep{rahman2025, zhangY2025} and from runtime telemetry
\citep{rodriguez2024}. Bland et al.\ \citep{bland2020} build an attacker
that observes partial state, acts at a cost, and learns its strategy
against a dynamic defender: evidence that the
sequencing, adaptive attacker MTD evaluation omits is constructible, if
so far only on narrow, hand-built cases. Outkin et al.\
\citep{outkin2022} address the parameters: they fit an attacker's
per-step detection probabilities to MITRE ATT\&CK Evaluations
data.
The apparatus is in place; it has not been brought to the MTD
evaluations surveyed here. The claim is about MTD evaluation as
sampled, not about the field's attacker models at large; the
field-level diagnosis rests on the two surveys.

The literature does not formally define its attacker models
\citep[p.~8]{jalowski2026}; this research gap motivates the attacker
model defined in Chapter~\ref{ch:attacker-model}.

%  session): the naming clause "which we are terming the movement attacker"
%  DELETED --- the model is the APT attacker model, its chapter title, and no
%  naming sentence replaces it. The two later "movement attacker" in this
%  paragraph became "it". DRAFT STATE, ratify on read: the run of "It"
%  openers is the sweep's residue and is Marc's to reshape.]
The APT attacker model that we have produced is the model that the
research gap motivated; it aims to meet the axes from
Section~\ref{subsec:attacker-criterion}. It replaces the \emph{baseline
attacker}, the inherited scripted six-phase attacker
(Chapter~\ref{ch:background}); it adopts its action set, and its movement
is dictated by the generalised stochastic Petri net and by the attacker
model at runtime. It is built beside MTDSim: we are instrumenting our
existing attacker model with it, so that we can compare our results. This
is a proof of concept, testing whether behavioural fidelity changes
existing MTD evaluation.

% [2026-09-04, Marc: the identifiers appeared twice in P2 and did not
%  read right] the section key FOLDED into the three spine clauses ---
%  each identifier once, paired with the sections that answer it; the
%  figure sentence closes the paragraph. Deletion and merge of the
%  ruled text only.
We are grounding it in CTI to capture the behaviour of APT attackers in
the real world (\ref{sq:capture}, Sections~\ref{sec:technique-graph}
and~\ref{sec:attack-profiles}). We are distilling that behaviour into
structured representations that we can operationalise and join to our
existing simulator (\ref{sq:model}, Sections~\ref{sec:petri-formalism}
and~\ref{sec:execution}), to evaluate our defence mechanisms
(\ref{sq:evaluate}, Chapter~\ref{ch:experiments}).
Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline} is the pipeline that answers them.

% 1 unit (L0--L1). The campaign corpus and its snapshot; aggregating
% flows into tactic dependencies. Answers capture. Disclosures: what
% the graph cannot represent. (Consensus-thresholds disclosure removed
% per Marc's ruling, 2026-08-16 --- the lossless/views record lives in
% the repo, not the chapter.)
\section{L0--L1: Cyber threat intelligence to attack graph}
\label{sec:technique-graph}

% DRAFT STATE (2026-08-16): passes 1--5 COMPLETE; Marc signed the unit
% off at ~400 words (leave-and-forget until the next editing round).
% OPEN, recorded in the handoff: (a) the unit sits at ~1.6 ledger
% units --- a further cut or an explicit named overdraft at the ledger
% pass; (b) DISCHARGED 2026-08-20 --- app:cooccurrence is filled
% (Table~\ref{tab:preliminary-extraction}); its framing paragraph is the
% only prose still owed there, and the 80% CONFIRM is STRUCK, not
% resolved (see the note at the appendix chapter); (c) v19.1 pin still
% needs a home in sec:experimental-setup;
% (d) the cycles-preserved sentence was cut at pass 5 --- the L3 unit
% must now introduce loop preservation itself (readers default to
% assuming attack graphs are acyclic).
% FIGURE (2026-08-20, Marc's ruling): the three-panel figure that stood
% here is GONE --- it compressed 478 technique edges and 132 tactic
% transitions into two ~4 cm bands and read as a garble. The graphs it
% was trying to carry now live at full page size in app:full-graph,
% generated by tools/gap_appendix_figures.py; this unit keeps ONE
% parenthetical pointer, on the aggregation sentence. The two other
% panel refs were session-added parentheticals and are simply gone,
% restoring Marc's sentences as he wrote them.

We made the attack graph using Attack Flow. Attack Flow is maintained
by the Center for Threat-Informed Defense (CTID), which sits under
MITRE Engenuity \citep{ctid2025attackflow}. This is a project that
documents the per-incident behaviour of an APT attacker --- done by
adding sequence and dependency to APTs' known tradecraft. Attack Flow
maintains a documented attack-behaviour corpus spanning 2017 to 2024.
Other approaches were considered to produce this corpus for our
pipeline. Co-occurrence mining gave low coverage and keyword
mining poor fidelity (Appendix~\ref{app:cooccurrence}).
% CONFIRM DISCHARGED (2026-08-20). Appendix~D now carries the entry as one
% table. Neither number this comment asked for survived contact with the
% record: the 80% threshold is in no committed artefact (see the appendix
% chapter's note), and "single-digit edge counts" matches nothing --- the
% runs produced 42 and 60 technique edges. Both are struck rather than
% pinned, and the appendix deliberately quotes NO threshold.
%
% What the appendix DOES license, read at tactic resolution --- which is
% where "low coverage" is true, and it is true relative to Attack Flow, as
% Marc means it: of the 182 transitions 14 tactics admit, co-occurrence
% recovered 19 (10%) and keyword extraction 25 (14%), against Attack Flow's
% 91 (50%) on the same build and 113 (62%) in the graph that ships.
% So the sentence above stands as written; the appendix supplies its scale.
%
% AVAILABLE, not taken (Marc's call at the next editing round): the one
% argument the chapter does not yet make is that co-occurrence takes its
% direction from an assumed tactic ordering, so it can express no loop at
% all --- while this graph preserves loops deliberately. That is a
% structural incapacity rather than a tuning complaint, and it is immune to
% the threshold question. One sentence, if the ledger can carry it.
NLP-based approaches we ruled out as a class: we judged the relatively
low fidelity of the threat intelligence they produce to be something
we could not defend over the life of the project
\citep{rahman2025, buchel2025}. The strength of using Attack Flow's
corpus is that a human analyst drew every dependency, so there is a
degree of fidelity that other approaches could not provide.

Instead of using each incident independently of the
others --- where there is little insight in the corpus --- we
aggregated the 38 usable attack flows
(Appendix~\ref{app:full-graph}). We
added the graphs together:
we stripped out all of the unnecessary details, such as specific attack
procedures or human analyst notes, preserving the logical AND/OR
structure. But aggregation makes the logical AND
vacuous, because there are many OR paths which an attacker agent
can traverse. Once we had decided on Attack Flow as the preferred mode
of CTI, we then had to decide what the correct resolution was (tactic
or technique). That is why we decided to prioritise coverage at this
stage, whereas in earlier stages fidelity was a large concern. We
leaned towards tactic, because 88\% of the technique-to-technique
directed flows came from a single incident. But once aggregated,
the tactic-to-tactic directed flows had much greater coverage, and we
judged this trade-off between coverage and fidelity was necessary to
capture the generalised behaviour of APT attackers. There is also
precedent for using tactics as the node granularity
\citep{rodriguez2024}.

The aggregate APT attack graph (L1) is a
weighted directed graph where the nodes are the MITRE ATT\&CK tactics,
and each edge is weighted on how many times it is observed in the
corpus. We are using
human-analyst-drawn dependency graphs, and this is sparse input
data. The edge weight is a recurrence value; therefore, we cannot rely
on these weights alone for attacker agent traversal. Instead, we treat
the L1 attack graph as a skeleton of behaviour.
% (Cycles-preserved sentence cut at pass 5 --- L3 now introduces loop
%  preservation itself; recorded in the handoff for the L3 drafting.)

Another limitation we encountered was an issue with a lack of
pre-intrusion dependencies: any tactics before initial access were
sparse compared to the rest of the corpus. Pre-intrusion activity
typically happens on the attacker's infrastructure --- infrastructure
setup, passive recon --- and is not detectable on the defender's side.
Hence, in the corpus, reconnaissance only has a mention in 10 out of
the 38 attack flows. But since we are producing an attack model for
defender evaluation, we will later have to draw these flows in.
This is persistent across CTI, so we
would have to address it regardless of our input.
% [RULED (Marc, 2026-08-16), do not re-flag: the defender-validity
%  sentence and the observability-boundary naming stay OUT --- the "so
%  what" is the synthetic pre-intrusion structure, the L3 unit's
%  disclosure; if the term is wanted, L3 or ch6 coins it.]

% 1 unit (L2). Objective over motivation; the four classes. Answers
% capture. Disclosures: what the partition produced, operator
% concentration.
\section{L2: Objective-conditioned attack profiles}
\label{sec:attack-profiles}

% ---- Pass 5b, 2026-08-17: Marc's further cuts applied --- the "idea to
% ---- capture" opener, the numbers clause, the second concession, the
% ---- skeleton close all gone; the concession reparented to P4 as his one
% ---- confidence sentence; the operator point as his one characteristic
% ---- sentence + the Conti example. Deletions + his dictated sentences only.

The literature tells us that APT attackers are not a homogeneous group,
and so they are distinguished by campaign-level intent. We cannot
produce an APT attack model that could be considered behavioural,
because the L1 aggregate carries so many different motivations and
objectives which influence behaviour \citep{alshamrani2019}.

Motivation is something that we cannot ascertain. We can see what an
APT group did; this is an analyst inference.
Although motivation is a taxonomy built into STIX and
well defined, it is left unpopulated in the ATT\&CK STIX data set, in
ATT\&CK groups and in Attack Flow. Objective then becomes what the
attacker has been observed to have done, and this is evident through
the attack flows that the analysts drew.

We are extrapolating the literature and saying the prefix is invariant,
so we are going to treat the prefix of attackers as invariant and we are
going to look at the terminal tactic \citep{alshamrani2019}. Attack Flow
is composite data, with structure and dependency manually drawn to a
high level of fidelity; however, coverage is an issue. Attack Flow also
uses and operationalises the MITRE ATT\&CK framework, but it does not
limit itself to its taxonomy, and so the attack flows can have nodes
which do not directly map to the MITRE framework and which the prior levels
in the pipeline removed. Some of the attack flows could be left
incomplete, and that is why we had to cross-reference the objective,
because we could not rely on the objective tactic alone.
% [3b, standing] "we are going to" x2; citation here and in P1; "removed".

Hence, we settled on a four-way partition based on the shape of our
corpus. Terminal tactic is the primary evidence for objective, but we
cross-checked against the CTID description, the ATT\&CK page and
vendor reports. That is why 19 of the 38 attack flows land in a
different category from the one defined by the terminal tactic alone
(see Appendix~\ref{app:classification-audit}). There are four, and from
here on they are referred to by index: $c_1$, the exfiltration objective (19);
$c_2$, the impact objective (7); $c_3$, double extortion, which we define as
containing both impact and exfiltration in the same attack flow (7); and
$c_4$, no realised objective (5). We have judged this partitioning
the right balance for corpus coverage: the smallest attack profile has a
minimum of five flows, which provides enough coverage. Six other
partitions were considered and dismissed
(see Appendix~\ref{app:rejected-partitions}).
% [CUT per ruling (pass 5c): the rhetorical question "is this the right
%      objective for the incident?"; the parenthetical "(impact,
%      exfiltration, both, or neither)"; the "Through this criteria ..."
%      confidence sentence (duplication). Confidence basis now implicit in
%      the cross-check sentence; 38/38 rides in
%      Appendix~\ref{app:classification-audit}.]
% [3b] "based on the shape of our corpus" --- still up for "based on the
%      terminal tactic".

They are not four disjoint graphs, because they come from the same
aggregate, which is written $c_{\mathrm{agg}}$ where it is run beside them.
They differ by what transitions exist. Granted, some of the
flows that come from the same APT attacker, where there are multiple
incidents, go to the same objective attack profile, and this shows that
the motivation of these APT attackers typically translates to similar
objectives. Three of the seven
flows in the double extortion profile come from one APT group, Conti,
which is known for double extortion, so we are not surprised there.

\section{L3: Generalised stochastic Petri-net formalism}
\label{sec:petri-formalism}

% ---- Marc's pass-5 compression (2026-08-18), written in with his rulings
%      on the session's scrutiny of it applied. Earlier pass records live
%      in the drafting handoff and git history. Order ruled: why GSPN >
%      the model > pre-intrusion solution > limits that carry to L4. ----
% [APPLIED 2026-08-18: topic sentence (b), Marc's own words from the first
%   dictation, chosen over (a) the question.]
We needed a data structure to pipe in these attack profiles from L2 as
an input, and this is what the Petri nets provide.
% [RULED 2026-08-18, do not re-flag] the 37% number stays out (not needed);
%   "declared and kept apart from the observed profiles" stays out
%   (implicit in "sub-Petri-net ... we join it").
We ruled out attack graphs, which we already have from L1, because they
do not supply the timing and stochastic elements we needed; DAGs,
because they are acyclic (the L1
attack graph is cyclic). The other forms of Petri net: an SPN does
not have the right mechanism, since it times every transition, and a
DSPN is over-engineered, adding a fixed delay we do not need, so we used
the one in the middle, a GSPN~\citep{marsan1984}, which provides the
immediate transitions we are using. There is also a large
precedent for stochastic Petri nets in the MTD evaluation
space~\citep{cai2016, chobenasher2018, mendonca2023}.
% [APPLIED, your ruling] the sentence head restored ("We ruled out attack
%   graphs, which we already have from L1, because ...").
% [APPLIED 2026-08-18] SPN / DSPN / GSPN-in-the-middle clause (yours).

% DRAFT STATE (2026-09-08): P2 is the FORMALISM, SESSION-PROPOSED on

% is the corpus's convention (Bland 2020 §2.1; Chiola/Marsan's 5-tuple in
% the ICS GSPN paper; Mendonça 2023 §2.3): attribute, state the standard
% tuple once, define this work's net as an instance with an enumerated

% (self-loops, one token, construct before execution, plug and play,

% §8 (rulings R1--R6). Tuple form: the compact (P, T_T, T_I, I, O, W, M0)

% slot is not wanted in ch4; [3b] "verdict" is the code's word --- keep,
% or "outcome". Symbols introduced here are the chapter's from now on:
% \mu_p, w_c, \tau_p, t_{pq}, \hat p, v, F_v, M_0; §4.4 re-keys to them
% in Marc's later pass. Bib: marsan1984 / marsan1995 are VERIFY stubs.

% standard tuple + firing semantics + unused elements named; the profile

% the symbols the chapter's vocabulary and points at Table 4.x. Marc's
% sentences reconstructed, not carried, on his licence. RULED 2026-09-08
% (Marc): "with the verdict"; the zero-factor sentence STAYS as the exact
% statement (a zero disables the transition for that firing only, so it
% cannot fire --- twelve cells of the failure matrix are zero). Open:
% the name "verdict-conditioned" (R2), the tex's own phrase from §4.4.2.
% §4.1--§4.2 are NOT re-keyed (Marc's question, 2026-09-08; session
% recommendation accepted in principle): the formalism begins where the
% executable object begins, at L3; L0--L2 are construction, described in
% prose, and their only formal-looking statement (the L1 graph) is
% informal by design. §4.3 reaches back with "each attack profile c of
% Section 4.2", which is the join. One §4.4 re-key item noted: §4.4.1's
% "base transition weights are recurrence values" must become w_c, the
% flow proportion of Eq. 4.2 --- the L1 edge weight is a count, the net
% weight a proportion, and §4.4 currently blurs them.
A GSPN is a directed bipartite graph of places and transitions in which
every transition is either \emph{timed}, firing after an exponentially
distributed delay, or \emph{immediate}, firing in zero time and with
priority over the timed ones \citep{marsan1984}. Following Ajmone
Marsan et al.\ \citep{marsan1995}, a GSPN is the tuple
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{N} = (P, T_T, T_I, I, O, W, M_0),
  \label{eq:gspn}
\end{equation}
where $P$ is a finite set of places; $T_T$ and $T_I$ are the disjoint
finite sets of timed and immediate transitions; $I$ and $O$ are the
input and output arcs, from places to transitions and from transitions
to places; $W$ assigns a firing rate to each timed transition and a
weight to each immediate one; and $M_0$ is the initial marking, the
number of tokens on each place. A marking that enables an immediate
transition is \emph{vanishing} and is left in zero time, the enabled
immediate transitions firing with probability proportional to their
weights; any other marking is \emph{tangible}. Inhibitor arcs and
further priority levels belong to the general definition and are not
used here.

Each attack profile $c$ of Section~\ref{sec:attack-profiles}, and the
aggregate $c_{\mathrm{agg}}$ by the same construction, compiles
to a GSPN $\mathcal{N}_c = (P_c, T_T, T_I, I, O, W_c, M_0)$, where:
\begin{enumerate}[nosep, leftmargin=*]
  \item $P_c = \{p\} \cup \{\hat{p}\}$: one tangible place $p$ for each
  Enterprise ATT\&CK tactic~\citep{mitre2026attackv19} present in the
  profile, and one vanishing place $\hat{p}$ for each, at which the next
  tactic is chosen;
  \item $T_T = \{\tau_p\}$: one timed transition per tactic, from $p$ to
  $\hat{p}$, whose firing is the attacker's execution of that tactic and
  consumes a stochastic amount of time; a tactic whose declared time is
  zero is immediate;
  \item $T_I = \{t_{pq}\}$: one immediate transition per tactic pair
  $p \to q$ that the profile's flows draw, from $\hat{p}$ to $q$; there
  are no self-loops, because time spent within a tactic is carried by
  $\tau_p$;
  \item $I$ and $O$ are the arcs these imply,
  $p \to \tau_p \to \hat{p} \to t_{pq} \to q$, every arc of weight one;
  \item $W_c(\tau_p) = 1/\mu_p$, where $\mu_p$ is the declared mean dwell
  of tactic $p$ (Section~\ref{subsec:dwell-times}), and
  $W_c(t_{pq}) = w_c(p, q)$, the proportion of the profile's flows
  leaving $p$ that do so along $p \to q$,
  \begin{equation}
    w_c(p, q) = \frac{\lvert F_c(p \to q) \rvert}
                     {\sum_{q'} \lvert F_c(p \to q') \rvert},
    \label{eq:base-weight}
  \end{equation}
  where $F_c(p \to q)$ is the set of flows in $c$ that draw the pair, so
  a place's out-weights sum to one;
  \item $M_0$ places one token on reconnaissance when the pre-intrusion
  overlay of the next paragraph is in place, and on initial access
  otherwise.
\end{enumerate}
$T_I$ is the set of pairs with $w_c > 0$. The implementation carries every pair the profile's Section~\ref{sec:attack-profiles} subgraph admits and gives the pairs its flows never draw a weight of zero; a zero-weight immediate transition has firing probability zero in every marking, and every factor below multiplies, so the two nets are the same object in execution. $\mathcal{N}_c$ is constructed before the simulator runs. The
construction is the same for every profile and only $P_c$ and $W_c$
vary, which is what makes the net plug and play: each attack profile is
an input, and structure and semantics stay apart. One token is a choice
about the attacker, not a property of the net. The attacker pursues one line
of the campaign at a time, so the AND structure Attack Flow preserves stays
inert here; concurrency is a parameter this thesis does not exercise.
% [2026-09-13: A7 sentences landed on Marc's ratification of the fourteen-item
%  proposal (chat, this date); source register: handoff 2026-09-08_ch4_s43_gspn_formalism.md
%  §7.2. Ratify-on-read.]

A GSPN's immediate weights are constants, or functions of the marking.
The attacker model of this thesis needs them to depend on what happened
in the simulator, so we extend the profile net with the verdict. A
\emph{verdict-conditioned} profile net is the pair
\begin{equation}
  (\mathcal{N}_c, F), \qquad F = \{F_v : v \in V\},
  \label{eq:vc-net}
\end{equation}
where $V = \{\text{success}, \text{failure}, \text{none}\}$ is the set
of verdicts the token routes on (success and failure as the simulator
returns them for the action a tactic dispatched,
Section~\ref{sec:execution}; none at a tactic that dispatches no
action), and each $F_v : P_c \times P_c \to [0, 1]$ is a declared
factor on tactic pairs. When $\tau_p$ fires and the verdict is $v$,
the immediate transitions out of $\hat{p}$ fire with weights
\begin{equation}
  W_c(t_{pq} \mid v) =
  \frac{w_c(p, q)\, F_v(p, q)}
       {\sum_{q'} w_c(p, q')\, F_v(p, q')}.
  \label{eq:routing}
\end{equation}
$F_{\text{success}}$ and $F_{\text{none}}$ are the identity, so on a
success, or at a tactic that dispatches no action, the token routes on
the corpus proportions; $F_{\text{failure}}$ is the failure matrix of
Section~\ref{subsec:failure-matrix}. Success needs no factor of its own:
every flow in the corpus is a campaign that got through, so the base
proportions already are the success policy.
% [2026-09-13: A4 sentence landed on Marc's ratification of the fourteen-item
%  proposal (chat, this date); source register: handoff 2026-09-08_ch4_s43_gspn_formalism.md
%  §7.2. Ratify-on-read.]
Places, transitions and arcs are
untouched; a factor of zero disables a transition for that firing only.
A timed transition may also be cut short by an MTD mutation, which
returns failure. Any further declared factor multiplies into
Equation~\ref{eq:routing} in the same way and renormalises with it;
none does in the runs this thesis reports, and each arm of
Chapter~\ref{ch:experiments} that adds one names it.

The rest of this chapter and the evaluation are stated in these terms;
Table~\ref{tab:gspn-notation} collects the symbols, and
Figure~\ref{fig:gspn-gadget} draws one tactic of one profile net.

  exponential delay of mean $\mu_p$; the solid bars are the immediate
  transitions $t_{pq}$, each carrying its weight. (a)~The gadget in
  general. (b)~The same gadget on initial access in $c_1$, the exfiltration
  profile: the eight successors are the pairs with $w_c > 0$
  (Equation~\ref{eq:base-weight}), listed by weight, and the column in
  blue is $W_c(t_{pq} \mid \mathrm{failure})$ of
  Equation~\ref{eq:routing}. Under a success verdict the base weights
  stand; under failure the mass moves to reconnaissance, from 0.062 to
  0.750, which is the foothold-gate rule of
  Section~\ref{subsec:failure-matrix} acting on the net.}
  \label{fig:gspn-gadget}
\end{figure}

\begin{table}[htbp]
  \centering
  \caption[Symbols of the attacker model]{Symbols of the attacker model
  and where each is declared.}
  \label{tab:gspn-notation}
  \tablestyle
  \begin{tabular}{@{}lP{8.6cm}l@{}}
    \toprule
    Symbol & Meaning & Declared in \\
    \midrule
    $c$ & an attack profile: $c_1$ exfiltration, $c_2$ impact, $c_3$ double extortion, $c_4$ no realised objective; $c_{\mathrm{agg}}$ the aggregate before partition & \S\ref{sec:attack-profiles} \\
    $\mathcal{N}_c$ & the profile net, a GSPN & Eq.~\ref{eq:gspn} \\

    $R$, $d$ & the rule kernel and the lifecycle-distance kernel whose product is $F_{\text{failure}}$ & \S\ref{subsec:failure-matrix}, App.~\ref{app:weight-sets} \\

% [APPLIED, your ruling] "(see sensitivity analysis ch6)" deleted --- the
%   closing paragraph already sends parameters to L4, which declares and
%   points at ch6.
% [RULED 2026-08-18, do not re-flag] the ownership fold ("supplies only
%   the structure ... policy emerges through execution; that is L4") CUT
%   by Marc --- reads as a limitation/characterisation; the ceiling
%   paragraph's "L4 deals with this" carries the boundary. ¶2 + ¶3 merged
%   (our model = structure + semantics) --- four paragraphs, matching the
%   ruled order. "Generalised" coexistence clause: not important.

The tactics reconnaissance and resource development, before initial
access, are not well connected in our attack profiles. We therefore drew in
synthetic out-transitions from reconnaissance to resource development to
initial access, as well as a backwards transition from initial access to
reconnaissance, so that we could model the runtime behaviour of the
attacker agent. This pre-intrusion overlay is a sub-Petri-net in its own
right: we join it to the attack profiles to produce the executable Petri
net.
% [PROPOSED 2026-09-08, session, ratify on read --- the overlay in the
% terms of Eq. 4.1; deletable without loss to Marc's sentences.]
In the terms of Equation~\ref{eq:gspn} it is a second set of immediate
transitions with declared weights: the whole out-mass of a place the
corpus left with no out-transition, and a share $s = 0.1$ otherwise,
the observed weights scaled to $1 - s$. We can assume that they perform these because we know they do
that (Section~\ref{sec:apt-survey}), and it is defensible because nothing detects pre-intrusion
activity anyway.
% [APPLIED, your ruling] PRE-ATT\&CK head dropped as lit-review background
%   (strom2018mitre / mitre2020attackv8 stay in the bib for ch3); the
%   fragment repaired by deletion only ("and these" removed).
% [APPLIED 2026-08-18] M6 justification sentence.

But there are limits to what the GSPN can provide: parameters (the
timings $\mu_p$ of how long the attacker dwells at a tactic; the
exponential defence of the dwell times; the mapping $\varphi$; the failure
matrix $F_{\text{failure}}$) and mechanics (the verdict $v$ by which $F_v$
reweights the out-transitions a token may choose at runtime, based on
the success of an attacker agent in the simulator; sink retrace policy).
L4 deals with this.

\section{L4: Joining the profile net to MTDSim}
\label{sec:execution}

The question for L4 is: what can the profile nets $\mathcal{N}_c$ of
Equation~\ref{eq:gspn} not supply, and how do we join this to MTDSim? The
Petri nets can act as inputs, but they are like source code: they have no
way of running. A Petri net can be run on its own, but that produces a
timeline of what the attacker did. The timeline is a dead end: there is no
input from the simulator. We had to produce a two-way join.
% [CUT 2026-09-08, both streams] the mutation example ("Suppose, for
%  example, the attacker is thwarted ... follow the next tactic. This was not
%  representative ..."): §4.3 carries the argument ("needs them to depend on
%  what happened in the simulator") and the example ("cut short by an MTD
%  mutation, which returns failure"). Reversible from git 9a333c48.

% [E2 SWEEP 2026-09-22: the three layer names DISSOLVED on the supervisor's
%  ruling (not layers; names only on a needs basis) and Marc's (no names).
%  What L0--L3 produce is the profile net; what MTDSim has is the baseline
%  attacker's verbs; what L4 declares is the join --- this section's own
%  heading. DRAFT STATE, ratify on read.]
Everything from L0 to L3 produces the profile net: the token is moving
through all these tactics. Within the simulator, MTDSim, the baseline
attacker has its verbs and has six phases, as outlined in
Chapter~\ref{ch:background}. To produce results, we have to join the two.
We scoped it down: for this thesis we adopt the existing verbs from the
simulator, and we built the APT attacker model beside MTDSim --- not
embedded in it --- so that we can evaluate the MTD mechanisms using
different APT attack profiles by construction.
% [rephrase applied per your ruling: the two "beside MTDSim" sentences folded
%   into one, "beside" carried once.]
The join carries three things: the dwell times, the mean dwell
$\mu_p$ of Equation~\ref{eq:gspn}; the tactic-to-verb mapping, $\varphi$;
and the failure matrix, $F_{\text{failure}}$ of Equation~\ref{eq:vc-net}.
% [spine sentence --- Marc's dictation 2026-08-19; moved up 2026-09-08 as
%  the section's contract, bound to the three symbols; "is the join between
%  the Petri nets and our simulator, and it" deleted (the sentence above
%  says the controller is the join), "(the stochastic dwell times)" deleted
%  (the parameter is the mean; the draw is what is stochastic).]
These are the three inputs we had to declare to join the attack
profiles to MTDSim. We chose each of them to the best of our judgement
after consulting the literature; whether any conclusion of the evaluation
depends on where a chosen value sits is the question
Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity} answers.
% INSERTION A APPLIED 2026-09-18 --- DRAFT STATE, ratify on read (Marc's
%  green light this date: "swept is wrong, we kind of just chose them ... we
%  chose them to our best of knowledge after consulting the literature").
%  Replaces "; each is swept in the sensitivity analysis (Section 5.1)."
%  Why: "swept" is the wrong verb for the mapping (a comparison against one
%  alternative) and for the nine failure rules (held); and it misreports how
%  the values were arrived at. The clause now names the question §5.1
%  answers. Prior wording and its 2026-09-13 note are in git (32e309bb).
%  Source register: handoff 2026-09-17_ch5_s51_sensitivity_overhaul.md D5
%  insertion A + S5 defect D-4.

\subsection{The runtime mechanics}
\label{subsec:runtime-mechanics}

We made all of the actions in MTDSim independent of each other, so that the
profile net supplies all the tactic timing of the attacker agent at
runtime, the firing time of $\tau_p$ (the numbered joins of
Figure~\ref{fig:runtime-loop}). A drawn dwell \emph{replaces} the dispatched
action's native cost rather than adding to it: an action consumes its
tactic's drawn time and nothing further.

The APT attacker model is given the targeted objective of
Table~\ref{tab:attacker-objectives}. It attacks a target host if one is
visible, and otherwise prefers the hosts nearest the target's level; the run
ends when a target is compromised.
% [2026-09-20: P2 of the §5.2 split-stream scrutiny (setup handoff §AL1 1b, §AM),
%  session-drafted, accepted by Marc in chat; PLACEMENT IS HIS. Chapter 4 never
%  said the movement attacker takes an objective of Table 2.5, whose caption
%  scopes both to the baseline attacker; §5.2 and Table 5.3 rest on it. Facts:
%  src/mtdsim/l3_simulation/movement/targeting.py (Brown's host-priority rule),
%  attack_operation.py:749-757 (the run ends on a target). Ratify on read.]
% [2026-09-08: ", and produced the join through the controller layer"
%  deleted (the preamble says it); "the firing time of tau_p" bound; the
%  figure pointer folded in from the cut loop paragraph. The "supplies all
%  the tactic timing" clause is the RESOLVED 2026-08-20 wording, untouched.
%  The second sentence is MOVED IN VERBATIM from the tab:dwell-catalogue
%  caption (session-drafted 2026-08-20, landed) --- the replaces-not-adds
%  rule is the clock, so it lives here, not in a caption.]
% [CUT 2026-09-08, both streams: "The loop runs as follows: ..." (75 words)
%  --- fig:runtime-loop's numbered joins and §4.3's routing sentence carry
%  it; "This is verdict-conditioned re-weighting ... encodes direction" moved
%  to the failure-matrix unit; the two sentences restating Eq. 4.4 cut.]

% FIGURE (2026-09-08): fig:runtime-loop RESTORED as its own float --- the
% 2026-09-05 fold into fig:pipeline reversed on the supervisor's verdict
% (handoff 2026-09-08_ch4_fig41_redesign.md, ruling R1). Generated by
% tools/runtime_loop_figure.py at natural size (16 cm, \footnotesize): the
% lower half of the old ladder inflated, the drawing unchanged, the L3 strip
% now the same net fragment fig:pipeline draws (the window rule is imported,
% so the two cannot drift). Regenerate, never hand-edit. Marc, 2026-09-08
% (pass 5): "blow it up and have it in 4.4.1" --- it opens the mechanics unit.
% CAPTION: the retired fig:movement-dataflow caption (git e714938c) carried
% forward, the fragment and badge placements updated. SESSION-DRAFTED,
% flagged for the voice pass.
\begin{figure}[tbp]
  \centering
  \includegraphics{fig_4-4c_runtime_loop}
  \caption[The APT attacker model's runtime loop]{The APT attacker model's
  runtime loop. The profile-net band shows a fragment of one profile's net
  (circles are tactic-places, the hollow bar a timed dwell, filled bars the
  weighted moves, the accented dot the token); each profile compiles its own
  full net. The join's three declared inputs appear as glyphs,
  not as the artefacts themselves (mapping \texttt{v2\_partial}, failure set
  \texttt{v4\_failure\_only}); the MTDSim band is inherited
  unchanged. Accented edges carry the net's traffic, grey edges the join
  to the simulator. The numbered edges trace one iteration: (1)~the token's
  selected tactic enters the join; (2)~the tactic's declared mean is
  drawn through an exponential and consumed as simulated time; (3)~the
  mapping resolves the tactic one way, to an inherited verb or to dwell-only,
  in which case nothing is dispatched and no verdict returns; (4)~a
  dispatched verb's success-or-failure verdict propagates up; (5)~on failure
  the failure matrix is multiplied onto the token's out-transition weights
  and renormalised, while on success, and after every dwell-only elapse, the
  base proportions stand and the join is bypassed; (6)~the token fires
  the chosen move and the loop repeats.}
  \label{fig:runtime-loop}
\end{figure}

Success or failure at runtime, the verdict $v \in V$ of
Equation~\ref{eq:vc-net}, comes from MTDSim and is propagated up.
An action fails if the preconditions of the action itself were missing ---
for example, trying to exploit a vulnerability without having scanned for
it. The same holds for the moving target defence: if MTD interrupts the
attacker, then whatever verb it is on fails. The join can tell an
interrupt from an unmet precondition, and it discards the distinction before
routing: the failure matrix is declared over one failure verdict, not two.
% [2026-09-13: A5 sentence landed on Marc's ratification of the fourteen-item
%  proposal (chat, this date); source register: handoff 2026-09-08_ch4_s43_gspn_formalism.md
%  §7.2. Ratify-on-read.]
But if they are on a
dwell-only, there is no verdict ($v = \text{none}$), so this is not felt by
the token in the Petri net. A mutation that lands during a dwell-only tactic
cuts the dwell short and costs the attacker the time, but there was no action
in flight to lose and no verdict to route on: the token still leaves on the
base proportions. At a dwell-only tactic the defence is felt as cost, never
as a change of route.
% [2026-09-13: A6 sentences landed on Marc's ratification of the fourteen-item
%  proposal (chat, this date); source register: handoff 2026-09-08_ch4_s43_gspn_formalism.md
%  §7.2. Re-landed 2026-09-13 (second pass) on Marc's framing --- a dwell-only
%  tactic exercises no capability, so a mutation there is cost only --- and verified
%  at movement/attacker.py: the dwell-only branch routes on VERDICT_NONE whether or
%  not the dwell was interrupted (the interrupt is tracked separately, for cost).
%  So the brief's A6 ("felt in cost, not routing") was right; the first landing
%  of this sentence, which read D-21's "read as a failure verdict" as routing,
%  was wrong and is withdrawn. Ratify-on-read.]
% [2026-09-08: v and none bound here --- this is the declaration
%  tab:gspn-notation promises for v in V; the mechanism gloss ": objects are
%  passed around in the simulator, and if the verbs have not been run, it
%  fails" deleted (the example carries it).]

We employed a sink-retrace policy, because the token would frequently hit
sinks and terminate the run --- a concession to the thinness of the corpus;
we retrace the edge travelled. The retrace is for a structural dead end, a
place the corpus gave no exit. A place whose every exit the failure matrix has
zeroed for a firing is a stall, not a dead end, and a stalled run ends.
% [2026-09-13: A10 sentences landed on Marc's ratification of the fourteen-item
%  proposal (chat, this date); source register: handoff 2026-09-08_ch4_s43_gspn_formalism.md
%  §7.2. Verified
%  2026-09-13: controller/outcome.py returns the empty out-set as the stall and the
%  movement driver ends the walk; the retrace is the S5 sink policy only. The
%  optional "no declared weight set produces one" clause was dropped because the
%  appendix does not carry the zero-stall count. Ratify-on-read.]
The simulator we are inheriting largely
models the confusion and disruption of the attacker through a 20-second
confusion penalty that hits the attacker at certain phases. How the defender
thwarts the attacker is an invariant of the simulator, so we maintain that
20-second confusion penalty. Not all the timing is supplied by the Petri
net: the confusion penalty is the exception. The same rule covers everything
else the simulator owns. The six verbs, the mutation schedule and the penalty
are the environment's, held identical for both attackers, so that a
difference between the arms is a difference in the attacker and nothing else.
% [2026-09-13: A11 sentences landed on Marc's ratification of the fourteen-item
%  proposal (chat, this date); source register: handoff 2026-09-08_ch4_s43_gspn_formalism.md
%  §7.2. Ratify-on-read.]
% [RESOLVED 2026-08-20 on Marc's wording: the unit's opening claims "all the
%  tactic timing", so the penalty exception no longer contradicts it.]
% [2026-09-08: "Another characteristic is that" and "inherently tied to the
%  simulator; it is" deleted (doubled clauses); "in the Petri net" deleted
%  after "sinks".]
% [RULED, do not re-flag] routing-ablatability must-carry CUT from this unit;
%   guarantee stays repo-side (attacker_state_seam.md); ch6 cites it there.

Nothing carries between runs. Each run starts from $M_0$ knowing nothing of
the defence: the attacker does not hold the mutation schedule, learns nothing
about it within a run, and carries nothing from one run into the next. That
is the scheme-aware adversary \citet{jalowski2026} asks for, and this model
is not one; Chapter~\ref{ch:futurework} returns to it.
% [2026-09-13: A12 paragraph landed on Marc's ratification of the fourteen-item
%  proposal (chat, this date); source register: handoff 2026-09-08_ch4_s43_gspn_formalism.md
%  §7.2. Verified 2026-09-13:
%  movement/learning.py holds a within-run belief about which destinations pay,
%  degraded on mutation; it never reads the schedule. Ratify-on-read.]

The ceiling of this thesis lies in the verbs that we are adopting
from the simulator: the attacker model that we produce can only be as good
as what we adopt. But it is extendable and modular: our profile nets can
be mapped to anything given an appropriate mapping, or a stronger underlying
attack model can be built that operationalises all of MITRE's tactics.
% [MUST-CARRY: the mapping as a chosen input, the standing caveat bounding
%  every claim in the results chapter (ch5 cites it). Moved 2026-09-08 to
%  close the mechanics unit; ", and that would produce a more useful attacker
%  model" and "We did not have the time to do this." deleted; "Future work
%  can look into changing the underlying action layer, but this has been
%  scoped out for this thesis" cut (the preamble's "scoped it down" and this
%  sentence carry it; ch7 has the future work).]

\subsection{The dwell times}
\label{subsec:dwell-times}

Each tactic consumes some time in the simulator, of mean $\mu_p$
(Equation~\ref{eq:gspn}), as the baseline attacker's phases did. We cannot
derive the dwell times from anything we have done up to L3. The dwell
times do not exist in the literature; they
do not exist in CTI vendor reports. Prior papers describe this as inherently
arbitrary: trying to put times on an
attack~\citep{bland2020, mcqueen2006, mendonca2023}.
% [citations wired, pass 6 (Marc's ask): the record's in-print arbitrariness
%  statements --- bland2020 "notional and randomly selected"; mcqueen2006
%  "somewhat arbitrarily, we decided"; mendonca2023 "? ... reasonably
%  estimated" (stochastic_timing_design.md 3.2(1)). Outkin 2023 is the
%  parameter-grounding precedent, a different leg --- parked in the bib.]
This is a parameter of the model itself, not an invariant.
% [CUT 2026-09-08: "There is no MITRE tactic mapping to time resource." (the
%  sentence before carries it) and "How long does reconnaissance take?"]

We derived our dwell times for the tactics from the dwell times of MTDSim.
What we could take from the six original verbs, we used
directly, and we extrapolated the rest. The 15 tactics resolve onto four
families. Two take the simulator's costs unchanged: the scan-shaped family,
one enumeration pass at 35 seconds, for the tactics whose work is
enumeration; and the exploit-shaped family, the median exploit time at
4.5 seconds, for the tactics whose work is gaining or reusing access. The
other two have no verb to supply them, so we extrapolated from the exploit
shape: the low-and-slow family at ten times it, for the quiet holding
tactics, and the objective family at eight times it, for the terminal acts.
We then chose a band around each of the four: half to twice the value, and a
quarter to four times for the low-and-slow family, which rests on our
judgement alone. The model therefore carries four free timing values rather
than 15, and those four are what Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity} moves.

%  execution shares the verb at 22.5 s, so the sentence holds as the
%  assumption it states, not as a universal.]

We are not claiming that the attacker dwell is exponentially distributed,
but the behaviour of APT attackers is stochastic: stochastic means it can be
measured, but it cannot be predicted. The exponential draw makes the times
stochastic (the base dwell times $\mu_p$ are not). There is evidence in the
literature for stochastic attack behaviour~\citep{holm2014, madan2004,
bland2020}.
% [RULED 2026-08-20: citation set kept at three --- each carries a distinct
%  leg (holm2014 empirical; madan2004 lineage; bland2020 method precedent).]

%  that?"; "The capability is provided by ... which" deleted so the draw
%  sentence stands on its own. The shape check (Erlang-4, same mean) is the
%  sensitivity analysis's, forward-referenced above.]

\subsection{The tactic-to-verb mapping}
\label{subsec:tactic-verb-mapping}

We needed a mapping from the places of the Petri net, or attack tactics, to
the simulator's verbs; we are calling this the tactic-to-verb mapping,
$\varphi$. There is no real mapping here, so we used our best judgement. We
use a direct mapping with certain constraints: a tactic can go to only zero
or one verbs, and a tactic can only execute either zero or one actions in
the simulator at a time, so this problem remains tractable. Where there is
no mapping, the tactic is dwell-only: it consumes time in the simulator and
dispatches nothing. The dwell-only is a concession: the baseline attacker's
verbs that we are plugging into have gaps; many tactics are
dwell-only (Figure~\ref{fig:controller-mapping}).
% [RULED 2026-08-20, do not re-flag: the zero-or-one constraint sentence
%  stays (Marc withdrew it as a pass-5 cut candidate; the black box's
%  re-proposal rejected 2026-09-08).]
% [2026-09-08: "The Petri nets do not map one-to-one." cut (the next
%  sentence says it); phi bound at the naming --- closes the notation
%  table's [3b].]
% [figure LANDED 2026-08-20 --- slot filled, the ref above now resolves.
%   Generated by tools/controller_mapping_figure.py from the registry version
%   data/ogasp/controller/mappings/v2_partial.csv, the pinned ATT&CK bundle
%   (names, axis order, version) and lifecycle_consensus.json (stage bands).
%   CAPTION IS DRAFTED, NOT RATIFIED --- yours for the voice pass.]
\begin{figure}[htbp]
  \centering
  % Natural size (conventions §h): the generator sets names at \footnotesize,
  % so 10pt in the tool is 10pt on the page. Condensed and the caption pared
  % 2026-09-05 (Marc: it is a mapping, arbitrarily chosen; the figure took
  % too much page). Ratify-on-read.
  \includegraphics{fig_4-4a_controller_mapping}
  \caption[The tactic-to-verb mapping]{The tactic-to-verb mapping
  (\texttt{v2\_partial}). Each ATT\&CK tactic dispatches at most one verb of
  the action set, named as in the simulator's code; the seven in blue against
  an empty slot have no mapping and are dwell-only. Bands are the lifecycle
  stages. Table~\ref{tab:controller-mapping} gives the reason for each row.}
  \label{fig:controller-mapping}
\end{figure}

We tried other mappings: forcing a total mapping was just another input
that did not work: it was running a tightly ordered finite state machine in
an unordered but stochastic manner (Appendix~\ref{app:experiment-one}); the
mapping is swapped in the sensitivity analysis
(Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}). We also considered using MITRE
Caldera~\citep{applebaum2016}, but this would introduce too much overhead.

\subsection{The failure matrix}
\label{subsec:failure-matrix}

The base transition weights are the recurrence values of the earlier levels
as the proportions $w_c$ of Equation~\ref{eq:base-weight}, and we assume the
observed out-transitions are the complete set of choices open to the token
--- an explicit assumption, since the Attack Flow corpus is all we have to
draw on, and a different corpus would give a different net. But a recurrence value does not reflect an attacker, because an
attacker can succeed or fail, and these reports only capture success ---
there is survivorship bias.
% [2026-09-08: w_c bound --- the re-key §4.3's comment demands (the L1 weight
%  is a count, the net weight a proportion); ", and a different corpus would
%  give different transitions and weightings" deleted (the corpus-variant
%  column of the sensitivity analysis carries the counterfactual). "These
%  values are necessary, but they are not sufficient ..." CUT (the next
%  sentence and Eq. 4.4 carry it).]

We employed a failure matrix, $F_{\text{failure}}$ of
Equation~\ref{eq:vc-net}, because this is the blind spot of the CTI. This
is a separate input, independent from the nets and from the simulator's verbs:
we did not reverse-engineer any of these values. We needed a 15-by-14
tactic-to-tactic failure weight matrix (excluding self-loops), shaped by two
components. The first is the failure rules, $R$, independently decided:
each tactic-to-tactic transition was placed into a given shape (the nine
rules A to I of Figure~\ref{fig:failure-weight-decomposition}(a),
Appendix~\ref{app:weight-sets}). The second is distance, $d$:
we used the literature and overlaid the attack
stages~\citep{hutchins2011, alshamrani2019, mandiant2013, chemat2024},
which gave the four broad stages of an attack --- preparation, intrusion,
post-intrusion operations, and objective. ATT\&CK imposes no order on its
tactics, so the order the distance term reads is imported from these
lifecycle models rather than observed in the corpus. Distance is the number
of stages a transition crosses. A move to the adjacent stage is not
penalised; a jump across two stages is scaled down by a rate, the same rate
whether the jump is forward or a fall back; and a jump of three stages falls
below a floor, which is where we treat a transition as too far to happen at
all and zero it out. The two rates and the floor are the only numbers in the
distance term.

%  where too far away gets zeroed out). Why: §5.1 ¶3 moves the two rates and
%  the floor, and this was the only place a reader could learn what they do;

failure weights; the two rates and the floor are moved in
Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}, and the rules are not.

This is verdict-conditioned re-weighting (Equation~\ref{eq:routing}); this
is what encodes direction. We judged these a plausible set of values to
feed into our attacker model, and Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity} reports what
it would cost to be wrong about them.

% CONTENT POINTS, per metric (for Marc's dictation, not drafted here): the name

% reason it is new. Names and dispositions wait on the brief's rulings pass.
%  CITED (the field's; defined here only where this thesis fixes a checkpoint):
%   - attack success probability (ASP, Cho; Zaffarano's Success): the share of
%     runs that compromise the target host. NOT "ASR" after Ho, whose ASR is
%     hosts compromised per attempted action --- same letters, another quantity.
%   - network compromise ratio: hosts compromised by the end of a run, over the
%     network's hosts.
%   - mean time to compromise: the checkpoint this thesis reads it at (the first
%     host) and why, since the lineage's checkpoint (a compromise ratio of 0.8)
%     is one the APT attacker model does not reach; the share of runs that never
%     compromise a host is stated with it. NOT the inherited evaluation
%     function's quantity (metrics_semantics.md §a).
%   - with and without a defence: NCR reduction, 1 - NCR_defence / NCR_none
%     (RULED 2026-09-23, replaces "suppression"; same numbers), in the form of
%     Alavizadeh et al.'s mitigation factor and Sharma et al.'s reduction
%     percentage --- an operator on a cited metric, not a new metric. One clause on
%     why it is the compromise ratio and not the success rate: with a defence
%     running, the APT attacker model's success rate is zero on most conditions.
%  INTRODUCED (defined in full here, with %   - STEALTH, two field names, both adapted (2026-09-23 census; "attack
%     intensity" after He is WITHDRAWN --- RI is He's maliciousness metric):
%     attack rate / attack inter-arrival time (Zhan et al. 2013; Pendleton et
%     al. 2016, read there as aggressiveness): the attacker's actions per unit of
%     its ACTIVE time (the horizon would hide the contrast, because the baseline
%     attacker stops when it takes the target); and attack confidentiality
%     (Zaffarano et al. 2015, already in Table 3.1): the share of actions a
%     declared detector does not expose. The detector is the detectability
%     level D at the bare standard: D(t) = sum_i exp(-(t - t_i)/tau), every
%     action weighing 1, tau = 60 s (the one declared constant); an action is
%     exposed when D, counting it, reaches the alarm level theta, and theta is
%     swept on the figure's x axis. Why: the APT attacker "trades speed for evasion"
%     (Alshamrani, subsec:apt); slowing the rate avoids triggering a defence
%     (Ward, Duppel); fast scanning is easy to detect (Jafarian); none of the
%     three cited effectiveness metrics reads the evasion side. State the
%     detector assumed, and that a time-at-risk detector (Hong 2018's rationale
%     for ACD) would read the slower attacker the other way. The unit: one
%     action = one verb the attacker invokes, so the baseline attacker's
%     per-vulnerability rows count as one; a tactic that invokes no verb adds
%     time and no action.
tion.
%   - the §5.2 descriptive instruments (no field metric exists for the

% fidelity verdict EMERGES from the results and is read in the discussion
% (sec:fidelity-verdict, its table). Strands are clustered by INSTRUMENTED
% METRIC FAMILY (Cho's purpose axis, Table 3.1), not by experiment family.
% Section titles below are SCAFFOLDING --- Marc's to rename; the
% dimensions and the strands are fleshed out later. Supersedes the two-limb
% recommendation of handoff 2026-09-08_ch5_ch6_structure.md (Marc's ruling
% overtakes it; that file is another session's, left for its owner).
% RETIRED LABELS (this commit): ch:experimental-setup, ch:results,
% sec:burden, sec:metrics, sec:families, sec:mtd-results,
% subsec:prior-model-results, subsec:fresh-results,
% subsec:supplementary-results --- every \ref re-pointed; the old units'
% skeleton notes are kept at the unit that absorbs them.
% Chapter opening (unnumbered, owed, Marc's dictation): what this chapter
% does with the attacker model --- evaluate is the manipulation of the L4
% object and carries no layer number (ladder ruling, 2026-08-16). The

% DESIGN PASS 2026-09-09 (Marc's ruling: "put it into the tex, placeholder
% per section, roughly where it should be, what it should look like based
% on what we found"). The shape below is designed against a section-level
% anatomy of 25 MTD-evaluation papers, distilled into
% docs/workflows/evaluation_conventions.md; the design and its open
% rulings (C1-C8) are docs/handoffs/2026-09-09_ch5_experiments_design.md.
% Load the conventions file before drafting any section here.
%
% THREE FINDINGS THAT SET THIS CHAPTER'S SHAPE, so no session re-derives
% them:
%  (i)  One movement, not two chapters, is the field's dominant form
%       (conventions a) --- the merge ruling is ratified, not merely
%       permitted. Organising results by metric family is the right axis
%       when the contribution is the ATTACKER (conventions b), and Tay's
%       thesis already splits numbers from interpretation exactly as
%       ch5/ch6 do (conventions h).
%  (ii) THE FUNNEL (conventions f, He 2025): characterise the attacker on
%       the UNDEFENDED target first, prune to what matters, then evaluate
%       the defence against that. This is what lets one run set answer
%       both questions --- the MTD evaluation AND what the model captures.
%       It is why sec:effectiveness opens on the no-defence arm.
% (iii) "BASELINE" NAMES TWO THINGS and this chapter must keep them apart
%       (conventions f2): the NO-DEFENCE CONDITION is the reference every
%       effectiveness number is a difference from (Zaffarano makes this
%       definitional; Brown plots it as "None"; Tay normalises by it); the
%       INHERITED ATTACKER is the comparison arm. "The movement attacker
%       is the new baseline" is a ruling about the second and does not
%       remove the need for the first. Never let one word carry both.
%
% UNIT BUDGET (12, the ledger's): opening ~0.5 from chapter slack;
% sec:sensitivity 3 (preamble+register 1, then 0.75/0.5/0.75);
% sec:dimensions 2; sec:effectiveness 3 (one per subsection);
% sec:efficiency 1. REVISED 2026-09-09 second pass: sec:sensitivity 3;
% sec:dimensions 2; sec:attacker-in-operation 3 (one per subsection);
% sec:effectiveness 3 (one per subsection); sec:efficiency 1.
% Section and subsection titles are SCAFFOLDING --- Marc's to rename
% (heading conventions: sentence case, no acronyms, "movement attacker"
% is a name for the prose, not for headings).
%
% "ARE WE BUDGETING 3 000 WORDS FOR THIS?" (Marc, 2026-09-09.) Yes --- 12
% units at ~250 words is the ledger's own allocation for this chapter
% (docs/notes/_writing_guide.md), and his arithmetic is exactly right. Three
% things make that number behave differently here than in ch3 or ch4:
%  (1) FLOATS ARE OUTSIDE THE WORD COUNT. The ledger's targets exclude tables
%      and figures, and every caption in this chapter is written long on
%      purpose. So the chapter's ARGUMENT is mostly not in its word budget ---
%      which is what makes 3 000 words affordable for a results chapter, and
%      it is why the captions were drafted before the prose.
%  (2) THE BINDING CONSTRAINT IS PAGES, NOT WORDS. 15 figures and 7 tables is
%      about twelve pages of floats against roughly seven of prose. That is
%      above the corpus norm for an evaluation of this length (Brown two
%      figures and one table; Zhang about seven figures; Tay five and none;
%      Ho about eight and eight; Reti six and two). FLOATS.md marks 9 figures
%      + 4 tables CORE; the rest are the cut list, and they go first.
%  (3) THE HEADING COUNT ALREADY EXCEEDS THE UNIT COUNT: 5 sections + 9
%      subsections = 14 headings on a 12-unit budget. Under skeleton
%      discipline every heading is a claim on ~250 words, so the chapter only
%      balances if the two three-subsection sections (§5.3, §5.4) spend
%      NOTHING at section level beyond a roadmap sentence, and §5.1's three
%      subsections average two-thirds of a unit. That is the design's own
%      split (3 / 2 / 3 / 3 / 1) and it is sound --- but it means Marc's
%      instinct is right: MOST SECTIONS HERE ARE HALF-UNITS BY NATURE, and a
%      subsection that grows to a full 250 words of prose has taken the
%      budget from a sibling rather than found new budget. Watch §5.3.1 and
%      §5.4.2, which are the two that want to overrun.
%
% AND ONE RULING THAT RETIRES PART OF THAT (Marc, 2026-09-09): "I don't care
% how many floats exist --- I just need as many as relevant and reasonable and
% comprehensive enough." So the corpus float COUNT is not a constraint on this
% chapter and no float is cut to hit a norm. The test is relevance: a float
% that carries a claim stays however many that makes, and a float that only
% decorates a claim another float already carries goes, however few remain.
% FLOATS.md's list is re-read on that basis --- it is a relevance ranking, not
% a cut quota.
%
% ---------------------------------------------------------------------
% SCRUTINY PASS 2026-09-09 --- FOUR INDEPENDENT EXPERT REVIEWS, and three of
% their findings threaten a CLAIM rather than a presentation. Full record:
% docs/handoffs/2026-09-09_ch5_experiments_design.md §13, which carries all
% ten rulings owed (C19-C28). Read it before drafting any section here. The
% three that bind hardest, each VERIFIED against the code or the records:
%
%  (A) THE RUNTIME EFFECT IS REAL; ITS ATTRIBUTION IS WHAT IS OPEN. Corrected
%      2026-09-09 after Marc caught an over-compression --- the first statement
%      of this conflated two layers. AT RUNTIME THE PROFILES SEPARATE
%      DECISIVELY: between-class visit-stream divergences of 0.081-0.237
%      against null ceilings under 0.0022, which is 40-110x, and the record's
%      own words are that profile identity conditions the runtime visit
%      distribution far above seed noise. The runs differ, and no reviewer
%      finding touches that.
%      WHAT IS OPEN is whether the separation is OBJECTIVE CONDITIONING or
%      CORPUS SIZE: the profiles carry 19, 8, 6 and 5 flows, the size-matched
%      label-blind control has never run, and it is the only instrument that
%      separates the two. The same confound already fired the kill criterion on
%      the divergence-to-aggregate column, and it is why the aggregate is a
%      comparison rather than a clean ablation null. Separately, the
%      CORPUS-LEVEL check at L2 is the one that failed a size-matched null ---
%      a statement about the partition's structure, not about behaviour.
%      So: one arm to close an attribution, not a threatened claim.
%  (B) [RULED NOT AN OBJECTION, Marc 2026-09-09: disruption has to be
%      implemented somehow, this model is inherited and five or so papers use
%      it, and modelling it a particular way is not an error. Recorded because
%      the MAGNITUDE still bounds what the cross-arm sentence may claim, and
%      because it contains a live question --- see the handoff §14.2, where
%      EXPLOIT_VULN's uninterruptibility is classified as OUR design choice
%      (a consequence of the S3-R timing ruling), not a bug, but one that
%      diverges from documented lineage intent IS-INT-05/06.]
%      REGISTER CONSTRAINT THAT FOLLOWS, AND IT BINDS THE WHOLE CHAPTER: no
%      prose here descends into codebase internals --- no sentinel places, no
%      disposition numbers, no function names. A reader is being shown a model
%      of a network under defence, not this repository. State boundaries in
%      modelling language and leave the mechanism in the implementation record.
%      THE MEASURED ASYMMETRY, for the record only:
%      disruption_wiring.md §(d) measures the severance/re-roll split as an
%      INSTRUMENTATION asymmetry: per firing, application-class delivers
%      0.67-0.83 of native yield to this arm against network-class 0.92-1.00,
%      and the diversity family loses 89-97 % of its exploit-blocking windows
%      because exploitation is uninterruptible here --- a declared MAPPING
%      POLICY (D-35), not a defence property. Counterfactual from the same
%      record: make dwell-only places application-immune and the diversity
%      pair keeps 0.17-0.18, so most of what "application-layer MTD does to
%      this attacker" arrives through the DWELL sentinel. Nothing in this
%      chapter lets a reader separate the mechanism from the apparatus.
%  (C) THE DEFENCE IS NEAR-PERIODIC. [RULED INTO THE EXPERIMENT, Marc
%      2026-09-09: "we can add more stochasticity, that's fine, it can be part
%      of the experimentation." So the TIMING REGIME becomes a factor in
%      tab:factors-varied with two levels --- the quasi-periodic default and
%      true Exp(mu) --- rather than a limitation to disclose. The ch2 sentence
%      still needs correcting, since it describes a regime the default does not
%      use.] The default regime
%      draws expon.rvs(loc=200, scale=0.5): measured over 200 000 draws, mean
%      200.5 s, sd 0.50, CV ~ 0.0025, against CV ~ 1.0 for a true exponential.
%      Mutations arrive on a clock. `--timing-regime exponential` exists.
%
% AND THE GAP THE EARLIER PASSES MISSED ENTIRELY: there is NO MULTIPLICITY
% CONTROL anywhere in this chapter. Ruling the hypothesis tree "a planning
% artefact, do not surface it" removed the alpha-spending and the
% pre-registration along with the AND/OR gating, leaving ~160 leaf comparisons
% with no family-wise error rate and, as the only decision rule,
% tab:eff-conditions's "overlapping intervals are indistinguishable" --- which
% is not a test. Four sentences in sec:dimensions fix it, and multiplicity
% control can be surfaced without surfacing the gating.
% ---------------------------------------------------------------------

% THE STANDING TEST FOR THIS CHAPTER'S STRUCTURE (Marc, 2026-09-09, and it
% governs every later pass): "Don't put things in knowing the results, knowing
% that's going to be the strongest thing. Put things in that are strong on
% merit, strong in practice and strong in convention." The operational form:
%
%     WOULD THIS HEADING STILL BE HERE IF THE RESULT CAME OUT THE OTHER WAY?
%
% If no, it is fitted to an outcome and must be re-justified or cut. The
% distinction that makes this workable rather than paralysing: STRUCTURE is
% settled by convention and merit, CONTENT is left to the result. §5.1 closing
% by naming what its sweep selected is a convention (the corpus's strongest
% sensitivity analyses feed forward); WHICH parameter it names is the result,
% and the chapter does not know it yet.
%
% AUDIT AGAINST THAT TEST, run 2026-09-09. THE SKELETON PASSES: every section
% and subsection here is justified by a declared input of the model (§5.1.1-3),
% by the factor space (§5.2), by the contribution being an attacker (§5.3), by
% the two metric families the field's own taxonomy names (§5.4, §5.5), or by
% the comparison the thesis claims (§5.4.2). None of them depends on which way
% a number falls.
% ONE JUSTIFICATION FAILED AND IS REPAIRED: §5.3.3 was argued on "the measured
% negatives are among the most credible things the work owns", which is
% precisely a heading defended by a known outcome. It is now argued as the
% ABLATION SUBSECTION --- convention (Tay's evaluation chapter carries one, in
% this supervisor's lineage) and merit (an ablation asks what a component
% contributes, which is a different question from what the defence achieves).
% That justification survives whichever way every arm falls.
% THE CAPTION SWEEP THAT FOLLOWED: six captions stated a direction the chapter
% cannot yet state --- four of them from sweeps §5.1's own note says must be
% RE-RUN before they may be reported. All six now say what the figure is for
% and what would count as a finding, not what the finding is. Apply the same
% test to any caption added later.
%
% AND THE COMPANION RULE, because the first one is not enough on its own
% (Marc, 2026-09-09): "I know you've got lots of numbers but they could be
% stale." EVERY NUMBER APPEARING IN A COMMENT IN THIS CHAPTER IS PROVENANCE,
% NOT A VALUE. It records what some record said at the time it was written,
% under a configuration this chapter may not report --- ten seeds, the
% pre-restoration substrate, one MTD condition, a superseded timing regime,
% a mapping version since changed. It is there so a drafting session can find
% the study, never so it can be transcribed. NO NUMBER REACHES THE PROSE
% EXCEPT FROM A TRACKED ARTEFACT VIA A GENERATOR, re-derived at the
% configuration the chapter reports. The same goes for the direction of an
% effect: if a comment says a sweep was inert or a capability bought nothing,
% that is what was measured then, and the chapter states it only once it has
% been measured again.
% THE POSTURE THIS PUTS THE CHAPTER IN, and it is the right one: we have a
% model, we know what it declares and what it was given, and these are the
% experiments that interrogate it. What they will return is a hypothesis, not
% a memory.
% ---------------------------------------------------------------------

% SECTION CUT 2026-09-20 (Marc): the titled sensitivity-analysis section is gone
% and the chapter opens on the experimental setup. Three structural cuts
% produced no section because a body section this early has no argument to

% \S4.4 does) and V6 sends sweeps that do not fit the body to an appendix.
% The table leads app:sensitivity as tab_C-0a (label tab:parameter-register
% kept); ch4's five pointers now go there. Record: handoff
% 2026-09-17_ch5_s51_sensitivity_overhaul.md, "Third cut" and "Ruling".
% OWED (Marc): one sentence where the 200 s suppression figure is reported, or
% in sec:fidelity-verdict, that its size is exposed to the low-and-slow dwell
% (44 to 89 per cent across the band against 75 at the chosen value; interval
% owed from the analyser) with Appendix C as the evidence.

% 2 units + the factor table (tables are word-cheap). The factor space,
% set up and then run: the four attack profiles and the baseline attacker;
% the defence mechanisms and deployment strategies (ch2); network scale,
% density and MTD tempo; seeds, horizon, determinism, V4 steady-state
% reporting after the initial transient. The instrumented metrics are
% named HERE as instrumentation, grouped by family (effectiveness /
% efficiency, Table 3.1), with the comparability boundary
% (within-simulator comparison valid; cross-paper numeric comparison
% invalid) and the V1 hand-trace validation as disclosures --- the former
% sec:metrics unit folded in. The degenerate operating region that gates
% which tempos can discriminate (operating_point_discrimination.md) is a
% design fact stated once here.
%
% WHAT THIS SECTION SHOULD LOOK LIKE:
%  - [SUPERSEDED 2026-09-20: the sensitivity section was cut to app:sensitivity;
%    this section opens the chapter.] OPENS by picking up what sec:sensitivity selected (design C7b).
%  - A ROADMAP SENTENCE naming each following section's question and the
%    reason for the order (conventions b) --- universal in the corpus and
%    a house pattern in this supervisor's theses.
%  - THE RUN COUNT IS A CONSEQUENCE, NOT A ROUND NUMBER (design C8,
%    conventions d2). The MTD corpus does not address replication at all
%    --- Brown states no run count anywhere, Zhang and Reti report 100
%    because that is what they did. The method to cite is the confidence
%    interval with specified precision (Hoad, Robinson and Davies, now at
%    docs/sources/methodology/hoad2007_replications_wsc.md): declare the
%    error tolerable in the estimate, run until the interval is inside
%    it. Our own power arithmetic already does this and has no citation.
%  - THE MODEL DECLARATION SENTENCE to imitate is Barach's (conventions
%    d): repetitions, dispersion, intervals, and the test, in four
%    sentences. Ours differs in the test --- D-29 makes the arms
%    INDEPENDENT, not paired, so no paired test anywhere.
%  - NO LINEAGE PAPER REPORTS A CONFIDENCE INTERVAL. Reporting effect
%    sizes with intervals EXCEEDS lineage practice; claim it as a
%    deliberate improvement in one clause rather than slipping it in.
%  - [SUPERSEDED 2026-09-13: no ablation arms exist; the modulators are rows of
%    tab:factors-fixed at their inert settings, and the R3 dependency leaves
%    this chapter with them.] THE ABLATION ARMS ARE DECLARED HERE (design 3.3) and nowhere else:
%    the verdict-blind arm, uniform weights, the learning exponent and
%    decay, the incentive exponent --- each a capability with a null
%    value at which the run is bit-identical. They are factor LEVELS, not
%    a metric family, so they get no section of their own; their results
%    are reported in whichever strand instruments them.
%    DEPENDS ON §4.3 RULING R3 (show the history-term slot in the formal
%    definition). Without it the incentive rule and the learner have NO
%    ch4 antecedent, and two of the six ticked properties in
%    tab:fidelity-verdict are ticked on mechanisms the dissertation never
%    defines. R3 is load-bearing here, not a formalism nicety.
%  - VOLUNTEER THE CONCESSION (design 5b): the field's only enumerated
%    prescription for an evaluation metric is Jalowski's four guidelines,
%    and the third is that a metric "should compare the MTD scheme to a
%    state-of-the-art system, protected using the best available
%    methods". We compare mechanisms against each other and against no
%    defence, on one simulator, with the defender frozen. Own it in one
%    sentence here, beside the comparability boundary, and say in the
%    same breath which half of the attacker-realism demand the work
%    answers (CTI-grounded, objective-conditioned, adaptively routed) and
%    which it does not (scheme-aware --- ruled out of scope, future work).
%
% IS THIS THE "EXPERIMENTAL TREE"? (Marc's question, 2026-09-09.) The tree
% he describes --- branches by metric family, then the measures, then the
% configurations underneath --- IS the chapter's section structure, and it
% is already carried by the section headings plus the roadmap sentence. A
% tree FIGURE here would redraw the table of contents. What sec:dimensions
% owes instead is the FACTOR SPACE every section draws from, and the
% corpus's cleanest form for that is Reti's TWO TABLES: one of the factors
% that vary with their levels, one of everything held fixed with its
% value. Same swept-or-held logic as sec:sensitivity's register, applied
% to the experiment rather than to the model, and it makes the design
% auditable at a glance. Ho does the same across two sections; Zhang folds
% it into an untitled head before the first result.
% TABLES FOR THIS SECTION (2), no figures:
%   Tab. 5.2  FACTORS VARIED, with levels: attacker arm (inherited
%             baseline; four objective profiles; the aggregate envelope);
%             defence condition (none; seven single mechanisms; three
%             deployment schemes); mutation tempo; network scale and
%             density; the declared-capability arms and their nulls.
%   Tab. 5.3  FACTORS HELD FIXED, with values and a one-clause reason:
%             horizon, geometry, seed set, timing regime, mapping
%             version, overlay version, the confusion penalty.
\section{Experimental setup}
\label{sec:dimensions}

% RATIFIED 2026-09-23 (register E5; Marc: "literally it needs to be visible
%  that we're citing where we got the numbers from"; "that's fine"). Two
%  session edits: (i) "Fifty hosts" recast as "The network has 50 hosts" --- E5's
%  numerals rule, and a sentence may not open on a numeral; (ii) the third
%  sentence is new: the network is Zhang's second of four exactly (Table 2,
%  p.33: 50 nodes, 5 endpoints, 4 layers), and a database target is Brown's
%  Scenario 2 (p.4). The subnet count has no lineage precedent (Brown's 20 is
%  the only stated value) and rides "the simulator's own default".
\textbf{Network.} The network has 50 hosts across four levels of depth, eight
subnets and five exposed endpoints (Table~\ref{tab:experiment}), with the
network regenerated for each seed and the two database hosts at the deepest
level as the target. Every value is the simulator's own default
(Section~\ref{subsec:network-model}, Figure~\ref{fig:network-model}) but one:
the target set, narrowed from five database hosts to two. The hosts, levels
and endpoints are also the second of the four networks Zhang evaluated on
\citep{zhang2023}, inside the 25 to 200 hosts the lineage has run
(Table~\ref{tab:lineage-configurations}), and a database as the target
follows Brown's targeted scenario \citep{brown2023}. The configuration
never changes, so every difference reported is the
attacker's or the defence's.
% [3b] "narrowed from five hosts to two" --- the fact is verified: GEOMETRY
%  sets total_database=2 (src/mtdsim/l3_simulation/movement/run.py:67) against
%  TimeNetwork's own default of 5 (mtdnetwork/component/time_network.py:12), and
%  every other value in GEOMETRY is that class's default. The CHANGE is real and
%  is declared nowhere else in the document; the REASON is yours. If the set was
%  narrowed to make the objective a harder and more specific target, say so in a
%  clause here; if it came from somewhere else, say that. Do not leave it silent:
%  a target set that departs from the simulator's default and is not declared is
%  exactly what a reader checks.

\input{tables/tab_5-1a_experiment}
