\section{The generalised attack graph at both resolutions}
\label{app:full-graph}

% [prose slot --- Marc] the framing. What §4.1 cites this for is the
%   coverage-versus-fidelity trade: the technique resolution was tried
%   and discontinued, the tactic resolution carried forward. The figures
%   are ordered as that argument runs --- one incident as drawn, the
%   technique aggregate, the technique aggregate with the single-incident
%   tail removed, the tactic aggregate.

\begin{figure}[p]
  \centering
  \includegraphics{fig_B-1a_gap_flow_exemplar}
  \caption[One incident as the analyst drew it]{One incident as the
  analyst drew it: the CISA AA22-138B VMware Workspace ONE flow (TA1),
  reproduced from the Attack Flow corpus (v3.1.1, Attack Flow schema
  2.0.0) with procedures and analyst notes stripped. Eleven nodes and
  eleven dependencies span six ATT\&CK tactics. Each box carries the
  technique identifier, the technique name, and in grey the tactic the
  technique belongs to; the filled box is where the flow begins. The
  diamond is Attack Flow's AND operator: both branches into it are
  required before the flow continues. This is the input to the
  aggregation --- one such graph per incident, 38 of them.}
  \label{fig:app-flow-exemplar}
\end{figure}

\begin{landscape}
\begin{figure}[p]
  \centering
  \includegraphics{fig_B-1b_gap_technique_graph}
  \caption[The technique-level aggregate attack graph]{The
  technique-level aggregate over all 38 usable incidents: 124 techniques
  and 478 directed technique-to-technique edges (Attack Flow corpus
  v3.1.1; ATT\&CK Enterprise v19.1). Techniques are banded by their
  tactic, top to bottom in kill-chain order, and carry their identifier
  alone so that every box holds the thesis's smallest readable type; the
  band label gives the tactic and, in italic, how many of its techniques
  the corpus uses. Edge weight is the number of incidents that drew that
  edge: shading and thickness run from a hairline at one incident to the
  darkest at four, the highest any technique-to-technique edge reaches.
  Edges that run backwards through the kill chain are drawn in blue. A
  filled box is a technique some incident started at; a blue-outlined box
  is one some incident ended at. 419 of the 478 edges --- 88\,\% --- were
  drawn by exactly one incident, which is what the haze in this figure
  is: at this resolution the corpus is mostly single accounts of single
  campaigns rather than recurring structure.}
  \label{fig:app-technique-graph}
\end{figure}
\end{landscape}

\begin{landscape}
\begin{figure}[p]
  \centering
  \includegraphics{fig_B-1c_gap_technique_core}
  \caption[The technique-level aggregate above one incident]{The same
  technique-level aggregate with the single-incident tail removed: only
  edges drawn by two or more incidents are kept, and the fragments that
  leaves --- connected components of fewer than three techniques --- are
  dropped with them, leaving 35 techniques and 55 edges of the original
  124 and 478. Banding, shading and the entry and objective marks are as
  in Figure~\ref{fig:app-technique-graph}, with the identifier and name
  on one line now that there is room for them. An edge carries a number
  only where more than two incidents drew it; the unnumbered edges are
  all at the threshold of two. What survives is thin: four tactics
  (execution, stealth, discovery, command and control) keep five or more
  techniques between them and most of the rest of the kill chain reduces
  to a single technique apiece, which is what aggregating one level up is
  a response to.}
  \label{fig:app-technique-core}
\end{figure}
\end{landscape}

\begin{landscape}
\begin{figure}[p]
  \centering
  \includegraphics{fig_B-1d_gap_tactic_graph}
  \caption[The tactic-level aggregate attack graph]{The tactic-level
  aggregate --- the L1 attack graph the pipeline carries forward. Every
  technique-to-technique edge is rolled up to its pair of tactics, so a
  transition's weight is the summed observation count of the technique
  edges beneath it; the 52 transitions shown are those at weight four or
  above, out of 132 in the artefact (Attack Flow corpus v3.1.1; ATT\&CK
  Enterprise v19.1). States are laid out in kill-chain reading order,
  left to right and wrapping, and carry the number of techniques the
  corpus uses for that tactic. Shading and thickness run with weight, to
  a maximum of 26; transitions that run backwards through the kill chain
  are drawn in blue, and a dashed loop over a state is a transition that
  stays within the tactic. The three states drawn in grey ---
  reconnaissance, resource development and defense impairment --- have no
  transition at or above the threshold, which is the corpus's
  pre-intrusion sparsity: those tactics are recorded in incidents but
  rarely as a step some later step depends on.}
  \label{fig:app-tactic-graph}
\end{figure}
\end{landscape}

  \includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_B-6a_failure_weight_decomposition}
  \caption[The failure weight set decomposed into its two declared

  (\texttt{v4\_failure\_only}); panel~(c) is the set as it stands in the
  chapter, Figure~\ref{fig:failure-weight-matrix}. All three panels share one axis --- rows the
  source tactic $a$ on a failure verdict, columns the candidate next tactic
  $b$, grouped into the four consensus phases with the ATT\&CK reading order
  inside a phase --- and one grey scale from 0 to 1.
  (a)~The failure kernel: the value of the first of the nine declared rules
  that matches each pair, and that rule's key letter (key below the panels;
  rationale and tier in Table~\ref{tab:overlay-failure-rules}). The nine
  values are argued, not swept. (b)~The lifecycle-distance kernel $d(a,b)$:
  a function of the signed phase offset $\Delta = s(b) - s(a)$, printed as
  each cell's subscript. Its parameters --- the decay rates $\gamma$ and
  $\delta$, and the floor $z$ below which a factor reads as exactly zero ---
  are the swept set (Table~\ref{tab:overlay-distance-kernel}); the floor acts
  on this factor, not on a finished edge weight. (c)~The committed set, cell
  by cell (a) $\times$ (b): fourteen distinct values, twelve exact zeros, and
  every non-zero cell traceable by eye to one rule letter and one distance
  factor.}

  below it reads as exactly zero, which is what zeroes the three-phase
  corners of Figure~\ref{fig:failure-weight-decomposition}(b). Beneath the
  axis: how many of the 210 declared pairs sit at each offset, how many base
  edges with mass the five routing nets carry at each offset (323 in all),
  and $d$ at the declared point. No net carries a three-phase transition in
  either direction, so the floor has nothing to act on in this corpus and its
  sensitivity is zero by structure rather than by measurement; the offsets
  the kernel actually moves mass on are $\pm 2$ (15 forward and 12 backward
  edges). The point was declared from the lifecycle consensus and the
  persistence ruling before any run: the sweep defends it, it did not choose
  it.}

\section{The forced-total mapping experiment}
\label{app:experiment-one}

% [prose slot --- Marc] the framing sentences. What the chapter cites this
%   for is the "very poor performance" claim at subsec:tactic-verb-mapping;
%   [2026-09-08, pass 5] the chapter's "We expected the movement layer to
%   perform the same, or worse, because it is the same thing in proxy" was
%   CUT there --- an interpretive claim with no carrier until this slot is

%   what it is evidence OF is the two failure modes. Numbers to hand,
%   from the record's §3--§6, for whichever you use:
%     - friction: the exfiltration profile spends 95% of its action budget
%       on verbs the substrate refuses (aggregate 76%), because CTI tactic
%       order is not the substrate's precondition order;
%     - churn: the no-realised-objective and double-extortion profiles are
%       blocked 0% of the time and still fail --- 931 host-compromise
%       events across 10 runs landing on 22 distinct hosts, the same few
%       hosts re-compromised ~40 times each;
%     - effort does not convert to breadth: the baseline turns ~815
%       successful actions into ~40 distinct hosts (~20 actions/host); the
%       most active profile turns ~460 into ~2 (~210 actions/host);
%     - MTD moves neither verdict; the only metric that shifts is the
%       BASELINE's time-to-first-compromise (238 -> 356 s, ten seeds,
%       overlapping intervals --- directional only).
%   The caveats that travel with any reading of these numbers are the
%   record's §7: the mapping is a chosen input parameter and not a
%   fidelity claim; sink-censoring truncates two profiles' MTTC window;
%   one MTD scheme, not the family; ten seeds is reconnaissance, not a
%   powered study.

\section{Robustness of the declared dwell times across their bands}
\label{app:dwell-robustness}

% [prose slot --- Marc] the framing. What this section is evidence OF: the
%   declared values are arbitrary in the sense the chapter concedes, and
%   the question is not whether they are right but whether any reported
%   conclusion changes direction when they move across their declared
%   bands. Numbers to hand, from the record's §8 and §10:
%     - the grid: the four group anchors swept one-at-a-time over the
%       catalogue-derived bands against the MTD mutation interval,
%       1 740 runs, seeds (0-7, 42, 1234), horizon 15 000 s, mapping
%       v2_partial, overlay v2_lifecycle_distance, MTD scheme random;
%     - headline: no anchor band inverts any conclusion. The profiled
%       attacker is slower in all 130 cells, never once faster; no cell
%       where MTD helps the attacker with CI separation;
%     - the identifiability result worth stating: of the four anchors only
%       the low-and-slow one moves any outcome at all (Table~\ref{tab:anchor-sensitivity}),
%       and the two priced-by-MTDSim anchors are inert at both band ends in
%       both MTD conditions --- exactly the pattern the evidence badges
%       predict, arrived at independently;
%     - the standing qualification, and the most consequential sentence the
%       study produced: the evaluation's operating interval sits inside a
%       degenerate region where neither attacker completes the objective,
%       so ASR cannot discriminate there;
%     - the honest limit: the per-profile ordering is indeterminate at ten
%       seeds --- a power failure, not a parameter failure.

Each dwell family was moved as a whole across its band, the per-tactic
multipliers of Appendix~\ref{app:dwell-derivation} riding along unchanged, with
the other three families at their declared values. Table~\ref{tab:anchor-sensitivity}
gives distinct hosts reached at both band ends against the declared value under
each condition; Figure~\ref{fig:sens-dwell-anchor} draws the one family whose
band ends separate from the centre by more than a host.

\section{The exponential dwell family}
\label{app:exponential-shape}

% [prose slot --- Marc] the shape defence. The spine, from
%   stochastic_timing_design.md §3.1-3.3 and the note
%   docs/notes/ch4_methods/exponential_as_tractability_choice.md:
%     - what the exponential assumes: memorylessness, a mode at zero, and a
%       coefficient of variation fixed at 1;
%     - where it is defensible: the scan- and exploit-shaped tactics under a
%       retry-until-success reading;
%     - where it is plainly wrong: the low-and-slow group, whose defining
%       character is sustained, paced dwell --- mass concentrated AROUND a
%       value, which is the opposite of a mode-at-zero draw;
%     - why it is nonetheless the choice: the field defends the formalism,
%       not the shape. No surveyed stochastic-net paper defends
%       memorylessness as faithful to attacker behaviour, and the
%       substrate's own lineage switched to an exponential without
%       recording a justification at all. The claim here is a tractable,
%       precedented family, never a fidelity claim;
%     - the strongest available defence AND its named leak (madan2004: a
%       mean-time result can depend only on the sojourn means, PROVIDED
%       routing is independent of dwell --- which here it is not, because a
%       mutation races the dwell, so a long dwell is likelier to be
%       interrupted and shape re-enters through the interrupt channel);
%     - the counter-evidence, acknowledged not buried: holm2014's 203 025
%       intrusions find a Pareto best fit and report the exponential a poor
%       choice for time-to-compromise. Two qualifications travel with it:
%       opportunistic enterprise alarms rather than targeted campaigns, and
%       campaign-level rather than per-tactic timing.
%
%   The measured answer is Table~\ref{tab:shape-substitution}, and the claim
%   it licenses is deliberately narrow: the mean remains load-bearing across
%   the operating region and at every other cell tested, and it stops being
%   sufficient at the long-dwell corner under mutation pressure. That is
%   narrower than "shape matters" and stronger than the pre-S3-R sweep could
%   say. Worth stating plainly: the more behaviourally faithful shape is
%   WORSE for the attacker, because removing the exponential's near-zero
%   mass removes the short dwells that let an action complete between
%   mutations --- the property flagged as its least realistic feature is
%   quietly doing work.

% LEAD DRAFT STATE 2026-09-17 --- connective prose (green-lit), ratify-on-read.
The draw around each declared mean is exponential (Section~\ref{subsec:dwell-times}).
The substitution here keeps every mean and concentrates the draw around it (an
Erlang-4, the sum of four exponentials of a quarter of the mean), on the
low-and-slow family only, paired run for run with the declared draw at the
declared dwell and at the top of the family's band.

\label{app:decay-robustness}

% matrix's band-ends table, in the form tab:anchor-sensitivity already has, so
% that the body chapter draws no figure of a non-event. DATA: the weight
% sensitivity study RE-RUN at the reported configuration under the settled
% timing regime; the study on record
% (docs/implementation/pipeline/ogasp/weight_sensitivity_study.md) ran under the
% superseded fixed-dwell regime at ten seeds --- provenance, not values. The
% corner check on the influential pair is a row group here.
% [prose slot --- Marc] one framing paragraph: what is swept (the forward decay,
%   the backward decay and the floor over their declared bands, one at a time,
%   then the corners of the pair that proved most influential); what "inert"
%   means (band ends inside the interval at centre); and the honest limit the
%   record names (a finer per-profile ordering that fails to separate for want
%   of runs rather than of effect).
% LEAD DRAFT STATE 2026-09-17 --- connective prose (green-lit), ratify-on-read.
The distance term of the failure matrix (Section~\ref{subsec:failure-matrix},
Appendix~\ref{app:weight-sets}) has three numbers: the rate at which a jump
forward across two stages is scaled down, the rate for a fall back, and the
floor below which a factor reads as zero. Each was moved across its band with
the other two held, and the two rates were then run at their four corners
with the floor declared.
